| DL-0068 | 2026-09-21 | How many agent runs does a commit cost when readers have different access? | Superseded by DL-0077. **One.** The reviewer agent runs with a tenant-wide read identity, tags every finding with the exact set of sources it was derived from, and the platform filters per recipient (DL-0021). Narrower audiences are subsets of the widest run, not separate runs. | The naive reading of the owner's economist / finance-director / chief-executive example is one run per access level. Provenance tagging already makes that unnecessary — and the filter, not the agent's ignorance, is the control. This is the mechanism DL-0007 already assumed; stating it here stops a future implementer from running the agent N times. | `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | superseded — DL-0077 |
| DL-0067 | 2026-09-21 | How are the analysis files named, and does overwriting lose anything? | Fixed filenames per analysis type, **overwritten on every commit**. Nothing is lost: the analysis for any version is `git show {commit}:analysis/{name}`. | Accumulating timestamped files would duplicate what history already provides and grow the tree without adding a retrievable fact. Overwriting is what makes "the analysis as of that version" a single lookup rather than a search. | `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0066 | 2026-09-21 | Do images need git LFS? | No. Images are ordinary git blobs, with an allowed-type list, a size cap per image, **conversion to a web-renderable format on upload** and **metadata stripped**. Revisit only if a repository's size becomes a measured problem. | LFS adds a second store, a server component and a clone-and-backup complication for a corpus whose images are small and few. HEIC is the concrete reason conversion is not optional — no browser renders it — and stripping metadata is the same discipline the estate already applies to distribution-bound files. | `technical-design.md`, `prd.md` | partly superseded — DL-0242 |
| DL-0065 | 2026-09-21 | How does a user see where the system found something, and where paragraph numbers come from? | Paragraphs are numbered automatically within their section, written `1.1-3` and `4.3.2-6` — the hyphen separating the heading path from the paragraph index. Heading depth is capped at **four**. Numbers are **derived at render, never stored**, and appear in the PDF, the DOCX and the reading view. In the editor they render in a **non-editable gutter** beside the text, like line numbers in a code editor, recomputed as the text changes. Every finding cites a paragraph's **stable identifier** and displays its current number. | Owner requirement for addressing. Splitting the stable identifier from the displayed number is what keeps a finding, a comment or a cross-reference valid when a section is inserted above it — the same rule `writing-standards/methodology.md § Address content by name, not by position` states for our own documents. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0064 | 2026-09-21 | When does the platform suggest marking a named entity? | The unmarked-entity check is an ordinary heuristic, so it runs like any other: on demand mid-edit, and on the send-for-review screen. It reports candidates by paragraph number with the text it proposes to mark; the editor accepts or ignores each. Nothing is marked without a human accepting it — the entity layer stays AI-proposed, human-confirmed (DL-0005). It also feeds a standing side list of unmarked candidates. | Making it a heuristic rather than a special mechanism means it inherits the content keying, the caching and the audience rules already built, and the tenant administrator can attach or detach it like anything else. | `prd.md`, `review-findings.md` | active |
| DL-0063 | 2026-09-21 | (Refines DL-0044.) Are all generated views produced on every commit? | No. Two classes. **Mandatory** views are recompiled on every commit — the contradictions, ambiguities and blind-spot lists, the role and control directories. **Voluntary** views are generated on request; a job description carrying a role's rights and obligations is the example. A voluntary view may also **seed an authored document**: the platform drafts it, a human takes it over, and from then on it is an ordinary document with its own editing and review pipeline. **Job description** joins the document-type table. | Owner directive. The seeding mechanism is the general form of what the owner described — a human-authored document based on a platform-generated view — and it keeps ASR-09 intact: the generated view stays agent-only, and the document seeded from it is a copy under human authorship, not an edited view. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `backlog.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0062 | 2026-09-21 | Why do the ASR entries stop using tables? | Six-part scenarios are written as a bullet list with bold labels, not a Markdown table. | Obsidian's table prettifier rewrites cell padding between sessions, so a table produces diff noise on files nobody edited and breaks `Edit` anchors — a cost the brain already records at `cognitive-framework/methodology.md § UED 10`. A bullet list has no alignment to drift. | `asr.md` | active |
| DL-0061 | 2026-09-21 | When is the interface translated? | Ukrainian first; English translation is a backlog item, not a design constraint now. The architecture keeps interface strings outside the code (ASR-10); nothing else about i18n is decided. | Owner directive. The invariant is cheap to hold from the start and the work is not worth doing before there is a reader for it. | `backlog.md` | superseded — DL-0498 |
| DL-0060 | 2026-09-21 | How do documents link to one another? | A mention of another document **becomes a link when the reader may access the target**, and renders as plain text otherwise — the existence of an inaccessible document is not disclosed by its own link. Each document carries an **auto-generated "see also"** listing the documents it links to, filtered the same way. | Owner directive for the link; the permission filter is the agent's addition and it is the same principle as ASR-03 applied to navigation. A "see also" entry naming a confidential document leaks its existence exactly as a finding derived from it would. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | active |
| DL-0059 | 2026-09-21 | Where do images live? | An `images/` folder inside the document folder. A change to an image is **flagged in review and shown as before-and-after**, never left to a text diff. | Owner requirement — technical instructions carry many images. The review clause is the agent's addition: a replaced image produces a diff a reviewer cannot read, so acceptance would cover a change nobody saw, which ASR-04 forbids. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | active |
| DL-0058 | 2026-09-21 | How are documents organised for a user? | The repository keeps a **flat `documents/`**. The interface shows **folders with unlimited nesting**, held as Django metadata. Moving or renaming a document in the interface never changes its repository path. | Owner directive. Decoupling presentation from storage means reorganising the tree costs nothing in git, breaks no link, and leaves history intact — a document's identity is its folder name, never its location. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0008 (a document's identity) |
| DL-0057 | 2026-09-21 | Do documents have types? | Yes. A platform-administrator-managed table of document types: **policy**, **controls**, **technical instruction**, **notes**. The tenant administrator assigns a type to a document, and types filter the document list. A document needs no relatives — a technical instruction can exist with no policy beside it. | Owner directive. Worth keeping distinct: a **controls document** is an authored document with its own editing and review pipeline, while the controls **view** generated from a policy only reports what that policy already says. The two are not the same artefact and must not be collapsed. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — DL-0235 |
| DL-0056 | 2026-09-21 | (Revises DL-0050 and ASR-12.) When may a companion action run? | At any time, including inside an open editing session, and the editor chooses which actions to run rather than all of them. Results are keyed to the **content state** — a hash of the document tree — not to a commit. A repeat request for the same action against the same content returns the stored result. | The owner's mid-session requirement and the no-recompute rule are both satisfied by keying to content instead of to a commit: a commit is simply one content state among others. The send-for-review screen then shows results already computed during the session rather than recomputing them. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | active |
| DL-0055 | 2026-09-21 | How is a companion analysis tied to the version it describes, when the commit identifier does not exist yet? | **The analysis is written into the same commit as the content.** It is not a pointer that needs an identifier — it is part of the tree, in the document folder. The analyses are what the reviewer reads as the change's description, and the pending view shows them beside the diff. | The chicken-and-egg only exists if the analysis points at the commit. Both the content and the analysis are known before the commit is made, so they are written together and the question disappears. It also means the reasoning travels with the document into any clone or export. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0054 | 2026-09-21 | (Corrects DL-0048.) Is sending for review a separate act from committing? | No — **sending for review is the commit.** The button replaces Save. Pressing it shows the companion analyses for the current content; two choices follow: *continue editing* or *send for review*, and the second commits. | Owner correction. DL-0048 inferred a separate act from the stance name *my edits in review*; the stance is a view over committed-but-unaccepted edits, not evidence of a second step. | `prd.md`, `document-lifecycle.md`, `adr/ADR-0003-linear-edit-history.md` | active |
| DL-0053 | 2026-09-21 | Where do ideas that are out of scope but worth keeping live? | A `backlog.md` at the session folder root. The task-manager idea — generated views feeding work items — is parked there as the first entry. | The manual already had the artefact; the question was which existing home fits, not what to invent. A generated view is read to decide what to do next, so a work-item feed is its natural extension and belongs recorded rather than re-derived. | `backlog.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (the backlog's place) |
| DL-0052 | 2026-09-21 | Is an ASR document a required artefact, and what happens when one already exists? | Yes — the **Architecturally Significant Requirements** document becomes a required artefact type in the Engineering workflow, codified as a required artefact type with its own template. **One document per product, amended in place**, never one per session: entries carry a status and are superseded rather than overwritten, so redesign is recorded instead of erased. While a product has no stable home, the document lives in the active session folder and graduates later — the same rule already applied to ADRs. Each entry is tested against the six-part quality-attribute scenario; an entry that cannot be expressed as one is a **constraint**, which is a legitimate second shape and the test is what tells them apart. | Owner directive to codify. The six elements come from the SEI quality-attribute scenario and are canonical; using them as a **check** rather than a mandatory form is what keeps constraint-shaped ASRs from being padded into ritual scenarios. | `asr.md` | active |
| DL-0051 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q26. Supersedes DL-0008.) Where does confidential guidance live, and how is access granted? | Guidance is **an ordinary document in the same repository**, authored, edited and reviewed exactly like any other. Access is data: a table of `document, user, rights` with a full change history carrying timestamp and actor. **Tenant administrator and platform administrator** are the only roles that may edit it. | Owner directive. The separate repository existed to make least privilege mechanical against a user who could clone; with Django as the sole authorisation surface and no user ever touching git, that rationale lapses. Making access a table with history also generalises: every document's permissions become auditable, not only the confidential one. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `asr.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0007 |
| DL-0050 | 2026-09-21 | How may a user interact with the agents? | Superseded by DL-0056. Three constraints. **No free-form chat** — a user calls predefined actions only. **No repeated call for an unchanged version** — once an action has answered for a given version, it cannot be asked again, because the source has not moved. **Actions run against committed versions only** — the companion is unavailable inside an open editing session; the user ends the session, asks, reads, and may take the document again. | Owner directive. The third is the one that pays: analysis keyed to a version can be cached and never regenerated because a word changed, and the dedup rule in the second needs a stable version identity, which a commit provides and a live draft does not. This removes step 4 of ADR-0003, where the editor consulted the companion before finishing. | `prd.md`, `asr.md`, `adr/ADR-0003-linear-edit-history.md` | superseded — DL-0056 |
| DL-0049 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q18. Refines DL-0031.) What is the structure above a heuristic, and who sees what? | Three levels. A **heuristic** is one focused piece of prompt text. A **companion action** is a set of heuristics producing a user-facing analysis. A **side-document type** is a set of heuristics producing and maintaining a generated view. Visibility: the **platform administrator** creates and edits all three. The **tenant administrator** sees titles and descriptions of actions and side-document types and attaches them to documents, and **cannot see heuristics at all**. The **editor** calls actions and reads generated views, and sees neither heuristics nor the sets. On sending for review the editor confirms the set of actions applied and sees a report of what is being sent. | Owner directive. It reverses the part of DL-0031 that had the tenant administrator selecting heuristics: the tenant's lever is the composed action, not its ingredients, which keeps prompt text out of reach of anyone who could write to the test. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `review-findings.md` | active |
| DL-0048 | 2026-09-21 | (Refines DL-0039.) Is committing the same as submitting? | Superseded by DL-0054. No. A version is committed when the editor returns the document; **sending for review is a separate, explicit act** that marks a run of the editor's versions as ready. The reviewer agent runs on that send, never per commit, and the stance *my edits in review* exists precisely because the two are distinct. | The owner's own vocabulary carries it twice — a *sent-for-the-review state* in the agent model, and a stance named *my edits in review*. An editor commits several times before the work is ready, and pinging a reviewer on each would make review unusable. | `prd.md`, `document-lifecycle.md` | superseded — DL-0054 |
| DL-0047 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q28.) What are the stances called? | Ukrainian is canonical: the five Ukrainian stance names now listed in `glossary.md`. English follows the same logic: *Canonical version · Accepted · Pending edits · My edits · My edits in review.* (Which language is canonical: superseded by DL-0176.) | Owner directive, and the Ukrainian revealed the model. Four of the five Ukrainian names are built on the word for **edits**, not on document states. The user does not switch between five documents; there is one document and a filter over which layers of edits are shown, with authorship toggled on top (DL-0040). That is the tool being replaced, and the interface should follow it. | `prd.md`, `document-lifecycle.md` | partly superseded — DL-0176 (the language half) |
| DL-0046 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q31. Supersedes DL-0004; rewrites DL-0012.) LaTeX or Markdown? | **Markdown.** A constrained CommonMark subset is the canonical stored form. The manifest is `document.yaml` carrying title, language and the ordered section list. Entities are marked with attributed spans — `[CMO]{.role #cmo}` — never backticks, which mean *code* to every parser and export path. Heading numbers are a render rule derived from depth, never stored; cross-references point at stable section identifiers. Render chain: Markdown to HTML with a designer-authored stylesheet through weasyprint for PDF, and pandoc for DOCX. | Owner decision. The decisive argument is security rather than convenience: LaTeX is a programming language with file inclusion and shell escape, and compiling user-authored content server-side for many tenants is a sandboxing problem a Markdown subset simply does not have. The normal form survives unchanged and gets easier — blank-line paragraphs are Markdown's native rule rather than a convention imposed on it. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md`, `work/markdown-vs-latex.md` | partly superseded — DL-0100, DL-0126 (the manifest and cross-reference halves) |
| DL-0045 | 2026-09-21 | Where is the reasoning behind a change recorded, as opposed to the change itself? | Beside each document there are **document ADRs** — decision records generated automatically when a human changes the logic rather than the wording. The agent pre-authors one; the person edits it or leaves it. One ADR covers one decision, however many edits it causes: changing who approves a budget may touch fifteen passages across several documents, and they share a single record of who decided, when, why, and what the alternatives were. | The diff shows what changed; nothing in git shows *why*, and in a governed document the why is what an auditor asks for. Generating the record at the moment of the change is the only point at which the reasoning is still in someone's head. | `prd.md` | active |
| DL-0044 | 2026-09-21 | What lives beside the document, generated rather than written? | **Agent-maintained views**, recompiled on every commit, each with its own git history and no human editor: a **directory of roles** with their rights and obligations and links back to the sections they come from; a **directory of controls**; and standing lists of **contradictions**, **ambiguities** and **blind spots**. The lists are **document-wide and durable** — a working backlog editors and reviewers act against — not a per-commit report. Scope starts per document and extends to the whole repository later, since two policies can regulate the same role. For a holder of the guidance grant, each list also carries an **addendum** computed against the confidential requirements, visible only to them. | The owner's correction: a contradiction list regenerated per commit answers "what did I just break", which is the companion's job. What a reviewer and an editor need is the document's standing list of unresolved problems. Making the views agent-only removes the question of what happens when a human edits a derived artefact. | `prd.md`, `review-findings.md` | active |
| DL-0043 | 2026-09-21 | How is development staged? | Two stages. **Stage 1 — editor, diff viewer, commits, review and acceptance.** A working text editor that users can use the next morning. **Stage 2 — AI companion and reviewer, and the generated side documents.** Architecture is designed in full up front so stage 2 does not require rebuilding stage 1; execution is iterative from there. | Owner directive. It also puts real documents in front of real users early, which is the only way the document model gets tested before the agents are built on top of it. | `prd.md`, `RELEASE.md` | active |
| DL-0042 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q24 re-opened, and Q27.) Where do identity, access control and agent execution live? | **Identity and access control are Django's**, and **Forgejo is dropped entirely**. Repositories are bare repositories owned by Django; agent runs are container jobs the platform drives. ADR-0002 stands unchanged — this is its option (b), confirmed rather than revised. | The owner's own reasoning closes it: edits accumulate in the application and are committed on a user's behalf, so a forge cannot authenticate per commit and cannot hold the authorisation. Once identity leaves, and once the events that trigger agent runs are the platform's own, forge runners have nothing left to offer either. Nothing remains that a forge is specialised in. | `technical-design.md`, `adr/ADR-0002-django-owns-the-workflow.md` | active |
| DL-0041 | 2026-09-21 | How does a reviewer accept a run of edits? | The reviewer reads an agent analysis of the difference between the **last accepted revision** and the current one. Accepting makes the current revision the new accepted baseline. **Acceptance is recorded as a commit** to the document's **passport file**, carrying the reviewer's decision — so an acceptance always has something to commit even when no text changes. Where the reviewer wants corrections, the same commit touches the content files as well. | Owner directive, modelled on *accept changes* in the tool being replaced. Recording acceptance as a commit in the repository rather than only as application state means the approval history travels with the document, survives export, and needs no second store to be trusted. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0014 |
| DL-0040 | 2026-09-21 | Who can see the author of a change? | Everyone. Authorship of every change is visible to every reader of the document — this is not an anonymous system — behind a **show author** toggle so the diff can also be read without attribution. | Owner directive. Attribution is free: the commit carries it, and sentence-per-line makes it exact. | `prd.md` | active |
| DL-0039 | 2026-09-21 | (Resolves: Q30.) Branching or a linear sequence? | **Linear.** One editing sequence per document. No branches, no forks from a past point, no merges, therefore no merge conflicts. An editor takes the document, edits continuously, and returns it as one commit with an agent-written summary. Commits land in sequence and the reviewer accepts them in runs. Full record at `adr/ADR-0003-linear-edit-history.md`. Supersedes DL-0013, DL-0014, DL-0034, DL-0035 and DL-0037; restates DL-0028. | Owner decision. The side effect the owner named is the point: with no competing branches there is nothing to collide, and "all pending edits at this moment" becomes a plain read of the commits since the last accepted revision. | `adr/ADR-0003-linear-edit-history.md`, `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0038 | 2026-09-21 | (Supersedes DL-0001.) Where do the design artefacts live while the vision is unstable? | Superseded by DL-0124. All of them inside the active session folder: brief, ADRs, registers, decision log. No `docs/` tree and no release folder. The 0.1 release passport is parked at `RELEASE.md` until a real release folder exists. Moved with `git mv` so history follows. | Owner directive — the product vision is not stable and the architecture work is still running, so a version-scoped documentation tree encodes a structure that does not exist yet. | every file in this session's folder; `CLAUDE.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0037 | 2026-09-20 | (Resolves: Q29 in principle; two details stay open.) How is concurrent editing handled? | Superseded by DL-0039. **Pessimistic locking per file.** A user takes a section file for editing and holds it exclusively; no one else can work on that file while it is held. A proposal still covers one document folder (DL-0014) and acquires locks **lazily**, per file, at first edit — a document-scoped proposal does not lock the whole document. Because no two open proposals can touch the same file, **merge conflicts are structurally impossible**: main only ever gains one squashed commit per approval, in approval order. The lock belongs to the **proposal**, not the person: the editor holds it while drafting, and the file's reviewer gains write access when it is sent for review, which is what ADR-0001 requires. | Owner direction, taken from the wiki experience. It removes three-way merge, conflict detection and the conflict-resolution interface — the largest piece of new work ADR-0002 created — in exchange for an editor occasionally waiting for a held section. The document already divides into owned sections, so the lock granularity matches the role table rather than being imposed on it. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0036 | 2026-09-18 | How does the platform show who is working on a document right now? | *Superseded — the mechanism described here is branch-and-proposal, abolished by ADR-0003 (DL-0039). How presence is shown under a linear sequence is open.* Presence is derived, not declared: a branch carrying **at least one save** makes its author appear under *currently editing*, scoped to the section files touched. A branch with no commits does not count. Until a proposal is sent for review the **sense** of the edits stays invisible; only their existence is shown. | Everything lives in one repository per document, so the platform can compute this without anyone reporting status. Showing existence without content is the boundary that prevents collision without leaking unfinished thought. | `prd.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0006 |
| DL-0035 | 2026-09-18 | What happens between sending for review and the first human reaction? | Superseded by DL-0039. Sending with no saves triggers an automatic save first, then opens the proposal. The companion generates a **length-limited description** of the changes, which the editor may accept or edit. While **no human reviewer has acted**, the editor may amend the proposal and re-send, or cancel it and return to editing. Once a human reviewer has acted, amendments are allowed but cancellation requires that reviewer's consent, and the reviewer is notified of each amendment. The reviewer agent re-runs **on send and re-send only**, never per save. | Owner directive for the first three; the human-has-acted boundary and the re-run policy are agent proposals within it. A reviewer who has already read and commented has spent attention that a silent cancellation would discard, and re-running the agent on every save would multiply cost for no reader. | `prd.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0034 | 2026-09-18 | How do saving, undo and version navigation behave? | Superseded by DL-0039. **Save** commits, titled with the current timestamp; the button is disabled when nothing has changed. `<` and `>` step through the user's own saves. **Unsaved edits** is the name of the state the owner was reaching for — text changed but not yet committed. There is no uncommitted working tree on the server: unsaved edits live in the browser, autosaved into Django as draft state so a crash loses nothing, and become a commit only on Save. Undo inside unsaved text is delegated to the editor pane. In the pending stance, other users' unmerged proposals appear as **one squashed diff each** rather than as navigable timeline positions, with a visual boundary between merged history and unmerged proposals. | Owner directive. The draft-autosave clause is an agent addition: the tool being replaced recovers unsaved work after a crash, and a platform that loses an hour of typing because the user had not pressed Save would not survive the comparison. | `prd.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0033 | 2026-09-18 | What are the stances a document can be viewed in, and what are they called? | *Superseded — the stance names rest on merge-and-proposal vocabulary abolished by ADR-0003 (DL-0039). The current stances are in `glossary.md`.* Five stances, and the two approval gates give the first two their names — **Canonical** (approved as an edition), **Accepted** (merged after reviewer approval, not yet part of an approved edition), **Pending** (accepted plus every sent, unmerged proposal), **My draft** (the user's own saves, unsent), **In review** (what the user has sent). Structure is settled; the words are a proposal pending Q28, and their Ukrainian equivalents are part of that question. | Owner directive to define the vocabulary. *Canonical* and *Accepted* are named after the two gates the platform actually has — reviewer approval and edition approval — so the distinction is derivable rather than memorised. | `prd.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0032 | 2026-09-18 | What does each agent report on — the edit, or the whole document? | The **companion is edit-scoped**: it reports only on what this editor is introducing, never on the document's pre-existing backlog. The **reviewer agent reports both**, in separated groups. Mechanism: a **standing findings set** is maintained for the accepted state of each document and refreshed when that state changes; a proposal's findings are computed against its own head and diffed against the standing set, which classifies every finding as **introduced**, **pre-existing**, **resolved** or **aggravated** by this proposal. | Owner requirement: an editor asking for feedback on three sentences must not receive the document's entire unresolved backlog. The diff mechanism is the agent's implementation of it, and it is cheap — the standing set is computed once per accepted state and shared across every open proposal, not per proposal. It also gives readers a known-open-findings list on a canonical document for free. | `review-findings.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0003 (the proposal vocabulary) |
| DL-0031 | 2026-09-18 | (Refines DL-0016.) How are the review lenses managed and turned into prompts? | The lenses are called **heuristics**. Each is a database record with a title, a short explanation and prompt text. The **tenant administrator** selects which heuristics apply to the companion and which to the reviewer agent, and the system composes each agent's prompt from a platform-owned base plus the selected heuristics. This splits the administrator role in two: **platform administrator** and **tenant administrator**. The finding taxonomy in `review-findings.md` is the seeded catalogue each tenant starts from. Every agent run records the exact heuristic set and versions it used, and every finding carries the heuristic that produced it. | Owner directive. The run-provenance clause is an agent addition and is load-bearing for a governed document: a finding that shaped an approval must remain explainable months later, which requires knowing which heuristic text produced it at the time, not which text is current. | `review-findings.md`, `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (where the prompt text is canonical) |
| DL-0030 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q24. Supersedes DL-0006; retires the CODEOWNERS derivation in DL-0025; invalidates the tenant-to-organization mapping in DL-0003.) Where is the boundary between the git server and Django? | Option (b): **Django owns the workflow and accesses git as a library.** Forgejo is retained only for identity and agent runners — and what remains of it at all is Q27. Full record at `adr/ADR-0002-django-owns-the-workflow.md`. | Owner decision, against the agent's recommendation of (c). The agent's case for (c) overstated the cost of (b): the per-file approval ledger, rejection records, comment threads, findings and role assignments were already on the Django side, so what (b) actually adds is merge mechanics, ref lifecycle and a proposal model — not the four subsystems the recommendation implied. (b) also removes the seam risk entirely, since a second authorisation surface no longer exists. | `adr/ADR-0002-django-owns-the-workflow.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0029 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q13.) How is tenant isolation implemented in the application database? | **Schema per tenant** in PostgreSQL. Isolation is enforced by the database rather than by application code; migrations run per schema; platform-level tables live in a shared schema. | Owner decision. A missing filter cannot cross the boundary — crossing requires getting a whole request's `search_path` wrong, not one query's `WHERE` clause. | `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0028 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q19.) What does the document hold while a passage is disputed? | The merged text holds **what the reviewer approved**; the Statement of Disagreements holds the rejected variant. Nothing enters the document without approval. | Follows from DL-0024 — if the reviewer edits the wording and merges the edited state, the merged text is by construction the approved variant, and the editor's rejected wording is what needs holding elsewhere. Recorded as inference from the owner's own model rather than as a separate owner ruling. | `prd.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (the merge vocabulary and disputes) |
| DL-0027 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q22.) Where do comment threads live? | Outside the document: a thread store keyed to anchors resolved against the sentence-per-line source, modelled on the W3C Web Annotation Data Model's quoted-text and position selectors, with a snapshot exported into the document folder whenever an edition is frozen. Nothing is written into the document itself, whatever its format. | Owner approval. Commentor roles may not edit text, so no anchor marker can be placed in the source; anchoring has to be external in every viable option, and an external anchor plus an external thread is one mechanism rather than two. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0026 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q23.) How is a rejection recorded, and how does a dispute end? | Adopted as proposed, with one addition: a dispute may be **settled by agreement between the editor and the reviewer**, and exactly one wording survives. The decider is reached only on deadlock. A Statement entry therefore runs `open → agreed → closed`, or `open → escalated → decided`. | Owner directive. Escalating every disagreement to the decider would make the decider a bottleneck on ordinary editorial friction; most disputes end in agreement, and only the deadlock needs an authority. | `prd.md` | deferred |
| DL-0025 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q21; adopts the CODEOWNERS concept.) How does a section reviewer approve only their own section? | Superseded by DL-0091. Per-file approval ledger with one merge. The **reviewer of a section is the code owner of that section file**: granting the role grants ownership of the file. Django holds the role assignments as canonical and **auto-derives** the repository's `CODEOWNERS` from them, with an auto-derivation header forbidding hand edits. | Owner directive — the code-owner concept maps exactly onto the existing section-reviewer role, so nothing new is introduced. The derivation contract is declared at split time — which copy is canonical, and how the other is derived: Django is canonical, `CODEOWNERS` is generated, and the generated file is never edited in place. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0091 |
| DL-0024 | 2026-09-18 | Who resolves a conflict or an unacceptable wording — the editor or the reviewer? | The reviewer may **commit onto the proposal branch** and approve the edited state, rather than returning the proposal. *Return for revision* remains available for when the whole proposal is wrong rather than its wording. | Owner directive. In software the author resolves because the author owns correctness; in a governed document the reviewer is frequently the accountable authority, which reverses the allocation. Recorded in full at `adr/ADR-0001-reviewer-corrects-and-accepts.md`. | `adr/ADR-0001-reviewer-corrects-and-accepts.md` | partly superseded — promoted to ADR-0003 (the proposal branch) |
| DL-0023 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q25.) Is the pending-edits overlay in 0.1, and who may see it? | In scope. A new role is added: the **pending edits viewer**, who sees edits proposed in sent proposals before they are merged, bounded by their own scope — a section-scoped holder sees pending edits only for their section file. Until a proposal is **sent for review** its saves are private to its editor. | Owner directive. The visibility boundary is the send, not the save: an unsent draft is nobody else's business, and once sent, concurrent edits to the same line are exactly what the reviewer and the other editors need to see. | `prd.md` | active |
| DL-0022 | 2026-09-18 | (Extends DL-0021; refines DL-0018.) What classes of finding must the reviewer agent produce, and which are in 0.1? | A third provenance class is added: **conflict with the existing document base** — another document already governs the matter, or already states the contrary, and the edit overrides it knowingly or not. The full taxonomy is written up separately at `review-findings.md`. Scope refinement: **detecting** a cross-document conflict at proposal time is in 0.1, because the agent can read the corpus the reviewer may read. **Notifying the other document's owners** when this one merges needs the dependency graph and stays deferred to 0.2 per DL-0018. | Owner example: renaming an API method that other modules call. The two halves are different costs — a read of sibling documents against a dependency graph that must be built and maintained — and DL-0018 deferred the second without meaning to defer the first. | `review-findings.md`, `prd.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (conflict detection in release 0.1) |
| DL-0021 | 2026-09-18 | (Refines DL-0007.) What decides whether a reviewer-agent finding may be shown to the editor, and to a reader? | A finding is publishable to a given recipient **if and only if it is derivable from material that recipient may already read**. A contradiction between two sections of the same document is derivable from the document, so it is shown to everyone with read access to it, readers included. A contradiction with the confidential guidance is not, so it stays in the reviewer-eyes-only artefact. | The owner's two examples are the two classes: an internal contradiction ("X approves Y" against "Y cannot approve Y") carries no confidential information, while a conflict with a one-line requirement in the guidance repository reveals the requirement by the mere fact of being raised. Stating the rule this way makes containment a mechanical test on the finding's provenance instead of something a prompt has to get right. ADR candidate. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0020 | 2026-09-18 | (Resolves: Q20.) Is the administrator a role, and what else is missing? | The **administrator** is a separate role, tenant-scoped — confirmed. A new role is added: the **designer**, who **creates and edits** the organization's style documents — styles, fonts, formatting rules — **and applies** a selection of them to a specific document. Style content is the designer's, not the administrator's. The **decider**'s power is clarified: the reviewer may accept an edit, edit it, or mark it as a dispute; once a dispute exists and the text carries several marked variants, only the decider can resolve it. | Owner directive. The designer owns the style vocabulary end to end — authoring it at organization level and selecting from it per document. The administrator keeps users, roles, document sets and prompt copies, and no longer owns style content. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0019 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q20 in part.) Is the role set of DL-0017 complete? | Two roles are added and kept: **decider** — rules on entries in the Statement of Disagreements, choosing the surviving variant, scoped to a document; and **approver** — records the approval that makes a frozen edition canonical, scoped to a document. Owner's own terms are used. | Both were in the seed's business logic and dropped out of the role table the owner drafted; the owner restored them explicitly. Naming follows the owner's words, per the action-naming principle in DL-0017. | `prd.md` | superseded — promoted to ADR-0007 |
| DL-0018 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q17.) Is the cross-document graph in 0.1 or deferred? | 0.1 carries the entity layer and manually authored cross-document links. Automatic triggering of review on connected documents defers to 0.2. Derived rights and obligation lists ship as a read-only view over the entity layer, not as a workflow. | All three capabilities are differentiating and none is needed to prove the authoring-and-approval cycle works; deferring the automatic trigger keeps 0.1 a genuine walking skeleton. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0017 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q16 — the role-naming principle and board-approval mechanics; role-set completeness carried as Q20.) How are roles named, and how is board approval recorded? | Roles are named by the action they perform, never by corporate title. The role set is: reader, commentor of a section, commentor of a document, section editor, document editor, reviewer of a section, reviewer of a document. Board approval is recorded by one designated person; quorum and voting are resolved outside the software. | An action-named role survives an organisation restructuring its titles, and keeping quorum out of the platform removes an entire class of governance features from scope without weakening the record. | `prd.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (the section roles) |
| DL-0016 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q15.) Who owns and versions the shared agent system prompts? | Prompts are editable per tenant from the start; the platform's shared set is the seed a tenant's copy is created from. | Tenants share nothing but the prompts (DL-0003), and a tenant revising its own reviewer's questions is the natural extension of that — the shared set is a starting point, not a constraint. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0015 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q14.) Which system owns user identity? | Superseded by DL-0042. Forgejo is the identity provider. Django authenticates against it over OAuth2/OIDC and holds no passwords. | Forgejo already enforces the repository permissions everything else projects from, and the MCP contract — a connected agent holds exactly its user's web rights — is only cheap to honour with a single identity store. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — promoted to ADR-0002 |
| DL-0014 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q05.) What is a document, and what unit does a change proposal cover? | Superseded by DL-0126. Superseded by DL-0039. A document is a folder in the repository. It contains a main file that inlines the section files, the section files themselves, and any technical data and YAML entity files the document needs. A change proposal covers one document folder; section granularity is a permission and approval concern, not a separate proposal unit. | Owner definition. Section order lives in the main file, so reordering is a document-level change — which is why the folder, not the file, is the unit a proposal and its permissions hang from. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0126 |
| DL-0013 | 2026-09-17 | What vocabulary does the interface use in place of git terms, and what happens to history on merge? | Superseded by DL-0039. Unsaved edits (working tree), save edits (commit), send for review (open proposal), approve review (squash and merge). Before merge an editor can step back through their own saves; on merge the proposal is squashed, so only the final state remains on the branch. The proposal record is the permanent audit trail of the individual saves, reviews and approvals. | Owner directive on the vocabulary. The consequence worth naming: squash means the branch is not the audit trail, so proposal records and their commit refs are never garbage-collected — that is where the traceability the product promises actually lives. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0542 |
| DL-0012 | 2026-09-18 | *Superseded — LaTeX was dropped for Markdown (DL-0004, ADR-0003), so this question no longer arises.* Given LaTeX as canonical source, what serialization keeps proposal diffs readable? | Canonical normal form: one sentence per line, a whitelisted macro set matching the editor's construct set, and a server-side normalizer applied on every save before commit. One sentence per line is **mandatory at the filesystem level and never visible to the user**: paragraph boundaries are blank lines, and the renderer joins the sentences between two blank lines into one contiguous paragraph. | LaTeX-as-source is only reviewable if the source shape is deterministic — a WYSIWYG that re-emits paragraphs as single long lines turns any edit into a whole-paragraph diff, and free-form line breaking turns reflow into phantom changes. Sentence-per-line is the established practice for prose under git review. Agent decision, not owner-gated; ADR candidate once the editor's macro set is fixed. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0046 |
| DL-0011 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q12.) What is published as open source after release 1, and under which licence? | *Licence and timing superseded by DL-0162 — Apache-2.0, published while in development.* GNU GPL, after release 1. | The repository carries nothing confidential in its history — no tenant data, no credentials, no guidance text — and that holds as a property of how it is built rather than as a check performed before a release. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (the licence and its timing) |
| DL-0010 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q11.) Can an edition become canonical with disagreements still open? | No. Every dispute in the Statement of Disagreements must be resolved before an edition can become canonical. | A canonical document is the authority other documents and derived role/obligation lists are read against; unresolved dissent inside it would make that authority ambiguous. | `prd.md` | deferred |
| DL-0009 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q10.) What is a Statement of Disagreements, structurally? | A durable register attached to the document, holding each disputed passage in its competing variants until an authorised decider rules, and rendered as part of the document's record. | Disputes outlive the proposal that raised them and must remain addressable; a transient workflow state loses the history the governance process depends on. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | deferred |
| DL-0008 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q09.) Where does confidential reviewer guidance live? | Superseded by DL-0051. In a separate repository with its own access control, not in the document repository. | Makes least privilege mechanical rather than remembered, and keeps the confidential material out of any clone or export an editor can legitimately obtain. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0051 |
| DL-0007 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q08.) How is confidential guidance contained when the reviewer agent writes back to the editor? | The reviewer agent emits two artefacts: a full reviewer-eyes-only analysis, and a separate editor-facing note written under an explicit non-disclosure constraint. Only the second is published to the editor. | The agent must flag contradictions with material it may not quote; separating the two outputs at generation time is the only containment that does not rely on a single prompt getting it right in one pass. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | active |
| DL-0006 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q07.) Is Forgejo the system of record or the storage backend? | Superseded by DL-0030. Forgejo is the system of record for repositories, change proposals, reviews and repository permissions. Django orchestrates over its API and owns Syngraphē-specific state: editions, the Statement of Disagreements, the entity layer, and tenant configuration. | Reuses Forgejo's review objects, ACL and CI runners instead of rebuilding them; the cost accepted is keeping two permission models consistent. | `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0030 |
| DL-0005 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q06.) Who authors the roles/actions entity markup? | AI-proposed, human-confirmed. No entity enters the document's semantic layer without an editor accepting it. | Derived rights and obligation lists are consumed as authoritative; inference alone makes them unreliable exactly where correctness matters most. | `prd.md` | active |
| DL-0004 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q04.) What is LaTeX's role in the document model? | Superseded by DL-0046. LaTeX is the canonical source. Diffs, proposals and merges are expressed in LaTeX text; the WYSIWYG editor is a view over it, constrained to headings, lists, bold, underline and italics. No tables, no other complex formatting. Style-only changes are auto-accepted; the text's sense is the object of human review. Style linting is deferred to a later release. | Constraining the editor's construct set is what makes LaTeX-as-source reviewable — a bounded set of macros round-trips deterministically, and separating style hunks from sense hunks keeps the reviewer's attention on the only thing that needs it. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | superseded — DL-0046 |
| DL-0003 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q03.) Who are the first users, in which language, and under what isolation? | Interface language is Ukrainian. Each document carries exactly one language; the platform handles documents in other languages, English included. The platform is multi-tenant: a tenant is a fully independent group of users or a separate business, mapped to a Forgejo organization. Tenants share nothing except agent system prompts. | Sets i18n as a day-one requirement rather than a retrofit, and makes tenant isolation an architectural invariant rather than an application-level convention. | `prd.md`, `technical-design.md` | partly superseded — DL-0542 (the Forgejo mapping and the Ukrainian-only interface) |
| DL-0002 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q02.) Must release 1 carry the accounting-policy revision end to end? | No. There is no date commitment. Release 1 works against dummy documents; the real source document is provided once the platform is ready. | Removes MS Word import from the 0.1 critical path and lets 0.1 be a genuine walking skeleton rather than a production migration. | `prd.md` | active |
| DL-0001 | 2026-09-17 | (Resolves: Q01.) Where do the Discovery deliverables live? | Superseded by DL-0038. The Project Brief lives in the release folder. The session folder keeps the seed, the concept pitch, `SESSION.md`, this decision log and the open-questions register. | Owner directive. Keeps the version-scoped product documentation separate from the session's own working record, and puts the brief where later releases' briefs will sit beside it. | release folder created; `SESSION.md` | superseded — DL-0038 |
